"THE MESSIAH."

The Musical Society At The Parish Church Last Night.

. Last evening the Warrington Musical Society gave a performance of Handel's most popular oratorio in the Parish Church in aid of the local War Funds, the society's own vocal and instrumental members being, with slight ex- ceptions, alone concerned in what proved to be a thoughtful and, in. many respects, quite adequate interpretation of this seasonable work. As there was a full attendance of a sympathising public, it is to be hoped that a substantial sum will have been realised for charitable purposes. Despite the • difficulties of properly Tnacing the society's forces in a sacred edifice, matters went fairly smoothly, only in one or two in- stances, where inter-action between vocal, soloist and orchestra was important, was any- thing noticeably faulty. Mr. J. H. Draper was heard at his best in the numbers "Comfort ye," "Every valley," and "Thou shalt break them," which he sang with quite appropriate expression, but he scarcely plumbed the depths ; of possible devotional feeling in the passion music commencing "Thy rebuke" and ending with the aria "But Thou didst not leave." For the music falling to the lot of Mr. J. B. Poole his voice and style are well-suited and everything he did from "Thus saith the Lord" to "Why do the nations?" bore the impress of his intelligent reading and artistic powers of interpretation. Mrs. Smallshaw's rendering of the contralto solos was in many ways praise- worthy, the unusually bright tempo in which she sang "0 Thou that tellest" imparting to it a 'degree of animation which is only too seldom found, in its presentment. Nor was her singing of the devout aria "He was despised" less appropriately soulful and artistic, an obvious sincerity of manner and intention being always distinctly in evidence in all she attempts. The soprano and mezzo-soprano solos were divided between Miss Hewitt, Miss Emily Taylor, Miss May Atkinson and Matter Neville Davies. The last-named was entrusted with the singing of "I know that my Redeemer liveth"—an experiment which did not prove an unqualified success, even with organ accompaniment alone. Young Davies's voice is remarkably full and sweet, and he has doubtless been admirably trained, but, last evening, his voice seemed unfortunately to be in that critical condition of being impossible to hold in control. There ,were consequently some liberties taken with the tempo and• Some haltings in the attack, which proved somewhat disconcerting. The ladies just mentioned quite satisfactorily sang the solos distributed amongst them: Miss Hewitt taking "Rejoice!" with , its, preluding recitatives, and Miss Taylor and , Miss Atkinson the twin arias "He shall feed His flock" and "Come unto Him." A plea- sant variation from custom was the singing by the principal vocalists of the two quartets Since by man" and "For as in Adam," in which the voices of Miss Hewitt, Mrs. Small- shaw, Mr. Draper and Mr. Poole mingled with a nice balance and a pleasing unity of effect. The society's chorus and orchestra both did well, attention, being given in the massed voices to many niceties of expression. and some ,nuanees which proved as admirable in performance as they were highly creditable to all concerned in them. In the more massive numbers there .was • noticeable a most impres- sive solidity and a fine volume of tone,, despite the fact that the ranks of the tenors and basses have of late years been _so largely depleted. _ _ _

Mr. Crossley conducted, Mr. Payton played the organ parts in the recitatives, adding also his quota to the sonorousness of the big chor- uses, and Mr. Darby, played, during the inter- val, an offertoire on two Christmas themes, by Gui -

===== 0002601_19151225_art0297.txt | included=true | included =====
THE LEATHER TRADE.

Trade was moderate last week, says the War- rington correspondent of the "Leather Trades Review," since many people were either stock- taking or preparing for it, and were therefore only receiving goods for immediate need. For- tunately, some forward business was done, and a fair number of orders • are accumulating for delivery as January Ist. Naturally most at- tention is now focussed upon the 'trade Of the New Year, and the balance of opinion is cer- thinly for a brisk opening after the holidays. This is supported by the number of inquiries which are being made, which .are well in ex- cess of anything seen since the summer, and cover most lines of our production. A steady inquiry is made for Army bends, and with a fair proportion of the next two or three months' output already booked. I expect to see prices rapidly return to the old rates. The inquiry for light bends was equally strong, but with such e large proportion of light hides going for jug work, these bends seem even scare-ar than the heavies: 'Hides are dearer again, some markets showing an advance of more t.hz,n ner lb.. and during the week some ;Jig parcds of salted hides have been sold at fairly high prices.
